type: manual
title: "Homeowners and Dwelling Rating Manual"
---
# Homeowners and Dwelling Rating Manual







## Part 1 — Rating Procedure

**1.A** Obtain a complete submission before assigning a rating basis. Hold incomplete submissions from final rating.

Complete source data supports consistent classification. It also reduces unsupported premium changes.

Record the source of each material rating input. Retain the submission image or intake record in the rating file.

**1.B** Identify the property as owner occupied, tenant occupied, seasonal, vacant, or otherwise occupied before rating. Refer occupancy conditions that cannot be clearly classified.

Occupancy affects expected loss activity and eligibility treatment. Unclear occupancy can produce an incorrect rating path.

Document the reported occupancy and the source of the information. Note any referral decision in the rating file.

**1.C** Confirm the insured location before applying territory or protection treatment. Refer conflicting location information for resolution.

Location data drives the geographic rating assignment. An unverified location can affect multiple rating elements.

Record the location used for rating and any address normalization performed. Preserve evidence supporting the final location selection.

**1.D** Apply the territory assigned to the insured location. Do not substitute a nearby territory for convenience.

Territory assignment reflects carrier loss experience and exposure conditions. Informal substitutions undermine rating consistency.

Document the territory result returned by the rating system. Explain any approved override in the rating file.

**1.E** Classify the dwelling construction from reliable underwriting information. Refer construction that is mixed, unknown, or materially altered.

Construction classification affects expected fire and other property loss severity. Unsupported assumptions may misstate the exposure.

Record the construction classification and supporting evidence. Identify the source used when construction details conflict.

**1.F** Determine the dwelling age from a reliable source when age affects the rating result. Refer uncertain or materially renovated dwellings.

Dwelling age can indicate differing construction features and loss characteristics. Renovation information may change the appropriate classification.

Document the age source and any renovation details considered. Retain supporting records in the rating file.

**1.G** Identify the roof covering and roof condition before applying roof-related rating treatment. Refer roofs with unknown condition or material.

Roof characteristics influence weather loss susceptibility and repair expectations. Current condition is material when it affects the rating treatment.

Record the roof information used for rating and its source. Note any inspection, image review, or applicant statement relied upon.

**1.H** Verify dwelling use before selecting the rating basis. Refer incidental business, short-term rental, or other nonstandard use.

Use of the premises can change the exposure beyond ordinary residential occupancy. Nonstandard use requires underwriting review before rating is finalized.

Document the stated use and any referral outcome. Retain evidence supporting the selected rating basis.

**1.I** Select the applicable form based on the property type, occupancy, and requested coverage. Do not use a form solely because it produces a preferred result.

Form selection must match the exposure presented. An unsuitable form can create inaccurate coverage and premium treatment.

Record the form selected in the rating file. Document the facts supporting that selection.

**1.J** Establish the replacement cost estimate using approved carrier methodology. Refer estimates that conflict with observed property characteristics.

The replacement cost estimate supports appropriate valuation and rating. A deficient estimate can distort coverage adequacy and premium.

Document the valuation inputs used and the resulting estimate. Retain any supporting valuation report or worksheet.

**1.K** Review the dwelling limit for consistency with the replacement cost estimate. Refer material differences that are not adequately explained.

The dwelling limit is a principal rating input. Consistency between valuation and limit selection supports sound underwriting.

Record the dwelling limit selected and the reason for any variance from the estimate. Preserve referral approval when required.

**1.L** Apply the all-other-perils deductible minimum before finalizing the rating result. Refer requests that do not meet the applicable deductible treatment.

Deductible selection affects retained loss exposure and premium. Consistent application prevents unsupported rating exceptions.

Document the deductible selected and the source of the request. Record any approved exception in the rating file.

**1.M** Apply any separate catastrophe deductible only when supported by the insured location and selected coverage. Refer unclear deductible applicability.

Catastrophe deductible treatment must align with the exposure and rating rules. Incorrect application can materially affect the premium result.

Record the catastrophe deductible treatment used. Retain evidence supporting its applicability.

**1.N** Determine whether extended replacement cost treatment is available for the risk. Refer risks that do not clearly satisfy the required underwriting conditions.

Extended replacement cost treatment can increase claim payment exposure. Eligibility must be established before the rating result is issued.

Document the coverage treatment selected and the supporting eligibility information. Note any underwriting approval.

**1.O** Apply the coinsurance threshold in accordance with the selected valuation basis. Refer risks with uncertain valuation support.

Coinsurance treatment depends on credible property valuation. Unsupported valuation can impair the intended rating outcome.

Document the valuation basis used for coinsurance review. Retain the facts supporting the resulting treatment.

**1.P** Identify detached structures that require separate rating consideration. Do not assume all structures share the same exposure characteristics.

Detached structures may differ in construction, use, and loss potential. Separate review supports accurate classification.

Document each material detached structure and its reported use. Record the rating treatment applied.

**1.Q** Review swimming pools, trampolines, recreational equipment, and similar features for eligibility treatment. Refer features that create an unresolved liability exposure.

Recreational features can materially affect premises liability risk. Clear identification supports proper underwriting action.

Document the feature disclosed and the action taken. Retain any supporting inspection or applicant statement.

**1.R** Identify animals kept at the insured location when animal exposure is relevant to eligibility. Refer restricted, aggressive, or inadequately disclosed animal exposure.

Animal exposure may increase the potential for liability loss. Incomplete disclosure prevents reliable underwriting assessment.

Document the animal information provided and the source. Record the referral disposition when applicable.

**1.S** Review prior loss information before granting loss-sensitive rating treatment. Refer loss information that is incomplete, inconsistent, or unavailable.

Prior loss activity can indicate unresolved conditions or recurring exposure. Reliable loss information supports consistent rating decisions.

Document the loss information reviewed and its source. Explain any treatment applied to the rating result.

**1.T** Identify open claims, unrepaired damage, and known loss conditions before binding coverage. Refer any unresolved condition that affects eligibility or valuation.

Open conditions can alter the current exposure from that represented in the application. Binding without review can create unsupported acceptance.

Document the condition identified and the action taken. Retain supporting correspondence or inspection evidence.

**1.U** Confirm that protective devices are installed, operational, and appropriate to the credit requested. Do not apply a protective device credit based solely on an unsupported assertion.

Protective device credits reflect reduced expected loss potential. Unsupported credits produce inaccurate premium.

Document the device type and evidence of operation. Record the source supporting the credit.

**1.V** Verify fire protection information before applying protection class treatment. Refer properties with uncertain responding protection resources.

Fire protection conditions affect anticipated fire loss response. Accurate classification requires dependable location-specific information.

Document the protection information used for rating. Retain the source or system result supporting the classification.

**1.W** Review proximity to brush, woodland, undeveloped land, or similar wildfire exposure when applicable. Refer risks with material wildfire concerns.

Wildfire exposure may affect eligibility and pricing. Early identification allows appropriate underwriting review.

Document the wildfire characteristics identified and the information source. Record any referral determination.

**1.X** Identify coastal, flood-prone, wind-prone, or otherwise catastrophe-exposed locations before final rating. Refer exposures that require specialized review.

Catastrophe conditions may materially affect expected loss frequency and severity. Accurate exposure recognition supports the carrier rating plan.

Document the catastrophe exposure considered and the source. Retain any approved underwriting direction.

**1.Y** Apply age, construction, and roof-related factors only when the underlying property information supports them. Do not estimate factors from incomplete descriptions.

Rating factors must be supported by reliable facts. Estimates made for convenience can produce an incorrect premium.

Document the property facts used for each applied factor. Explain any approved use of alternate evidence.

**1.Z** Identify substantial remodeling, additions, or reconstruction that may affect valuation or classification. Refer projects that are incomplete or inadequately described.

Material alterations can change the current property exposure. The rating basis must reflect the condition presented for coverage.

Document the reported alteration and its completion status. Retain available evidence of the work.

**1.AA** Review electrical, plumbing, heating, and roofing updates when they affect underwriting or rating treatment. Refer systems with uncertain condition or material defects.

Building system condition can influence loss potential and repair severity. Reliable update information supports accurate risk evaluation.

Document the system information used and its source. Note any unresolved condition requiring referral.

**1.AB** Determine whether the dwelling is vacant, unoccupied, under renovation, or otherwise not regularly occupied. Refer conditions that do not fit standard occupancy treatment.

Reduced occupancy can increase the chance that damage remains undetected. Nonstandard conditions require deliberate underwriting action.

Document the occupancy condition and expected use. Record any referral approval or rating restriction.

**1.AC** Identify business property, business activity, or customer traffic at the insured location. Refer exposures beyond incidental residential activity.

Business activity can increase property and liability loss potential. Accurate identification prevents improper personal lines rating.

Document the reported activity and the underwriting disposition. Retain supporting statements in the rating file.

**1.AD** Review leased portions, room rentals, and shared occupancy arrangements before rating. Refer arrangements with unclear control of the premises.

Shared occupancy can change liability and property exposure. The rating basis must reflect the actual occupancy arrangement.

Document the occupancy arrangement and the source. Record the action taken after review.

**1.AE** Confirm that all requested credits and discounts are supported before applying them. Remove unsupported credits from the rating result.

Credits and discounts must reflect verified risk characteristics. Unsupported treatment reduces premium without a corresponding exposure basis.

Document the evidence supporting each credit or discount. Record the reason for any removal or denial.

**1.AF** Apply package, companion, or account-related rating treatment only when account information is verified. Refer account relationships that cannot be confirmed.

Account-related treatment depends on accurate carrier records. Verification prevents duplicate or ineligible rating adjustments.

Document the account information reviewed and the result. Retain the system evidence supporting the treatment.

**1.AG** Review prior carrier information when continuity or prior coverage treatment affects the rating result. Refer discrepancies that cannot be reconciled.

Prior coverage information can reveal gaps, cancellations, or rating inconsistencies. Reconciliation supports a reliable underwriting decision.

Document the prior coverage information considered and its source. Record unresolved discrepancies and referral action.

**1.AH** Verify mortgagee, additional interest, and other interested party information when it affects issuance processing. Do not allow interested party data to alter the risk classification.

Interested party information supports accurate policy administration. It does not replace underwriting evidence about the property exposure.

Document the interested party information received. Record any correction made during processing.

**1.AI** Use only approved carrier rating tools and current system workflows. Refer system results that appear inconsistent with the submitted facts.

Approved tools preserve uniform application of rating rules. Manual workarounds can create untraceable rating differences.

Document the rating tool result and any system message requiring action. Retain approved override support when applicable.

**1.AJ** Review all manually entered rating inputs before releasing the quote or policy. Correct apparent entry errors before completion.

Manual entries are susceptible to transcription and selection errors. A final review reduces preventable rating defects.

Document completion of the input review in the rating file. Record any correction that changed the rating result.

**1.AK** Refer any risk that requires a departure from standard eligibility or rating treatment. Do not implement a departure without authorized approval.

Exceptions require controlled underwriting judgment. Authorization preserves accountability for nonstandard decisions.

Document the reason for referral and the facts presented. Retain the approval, denial, or modified instruction.

**1.AL** Record the effective rating basis at the time coverage is bound or changed. Do not use later-developed information to obscure the original decision.

The rating file must show the facts available when action was taken. Clear timing supports auditability and claim review.

Document the information available at the point of decision. Identify later corrections separately from the original record.

**1.AM** Re-rate the risk when a material exposure change is reported or discovered. Refer changes that cannot be accurately classified.

Material changes can affect eligibility, valuation, and premium. Prompt re-rating maintains alignment between the record and the exposure.

Document the change reported and the revised rating treatment. Retain evidence supporting the updated information.

**1.AN** Communicate rating requirements through approved carrier channels. Do not provide internal rating rationale as policy language or insured-facing contract interpretation.

Internal rating guidance is not part of the insurance contract. Controlled communication protects the distinction between underwriting practice and coverage obligations.

Document material communications affecting the rating decision. Retain correspondence used to obtain or clarify rating information.

**1.AO** Resolve conflicting information before final rating whenever the conflict affects eligibility, classification, valuation, or premium. Refer conflicts that cannot be resolved from reliable evidence.

Conflicting information weakens the support for the rating result. Resolution ensures that the record reflects a defensible exposure assessment.

Document the conflicting information and the source of each item. Record the basis for the final determination.

**1.AP** Maintain a complete rating file that supports the selected classification, coverage treatment, and premium result. Do not rely on undocumented verbal assumptions.

A complete file allows underwriting, audit, and claim personnel to understand the decision. Documentation also supports consistent future servicing.

Record all material sources, decisions, and approvals in the rating file. Retain records in accordance with carrier practice.

**1.AQ** Perform a final reasonableness review before releasing the rating result. Refer any result that does not reasonably align with the documented exposure.

A final review identifies apparent mismatches between risk facts and rating treatment. This control helps prevent avoidable processing error.

Document completion of the final review and any corrective action. Record the reviewer’s referral decision when further underwriting action is required.

## Table 2 — Base Rates by Territory: HO-3

**2.A** Use the table to identify the applicable HO-3 base rate for the assigned territory. Territory-based rating supports consistent treatment of comparable property exposure. Document the territory assignment and selected base rate in the rating file.

**2.B** Confirm that the risk location supports the territory used for rating before applying the table value. Accurate territory selection is necessary to maintain rating integrity. Document the source used to verify the risk location and territory.

**2.C** Refer any risk with an uncertain territory assignment before finalizing the rate. Unresolved territory information may produce an incorrect base rate. Document the referral and the final territory determination in the underwriting file.

Where a row refers to the other structures limit, apply the factor to the premium developed before that adjustment.

## Table 3 — Base Rates by Territory: HO-5

**3.A** Determine the territory assigned to the insured location before selecting the HO-5 base rate. The table presents base rates by territory to establish the geographic starting point for rating. Document the assigned territory and selected base rate in the rating file.

**3.B** Use the entry for the applicable territory when rating an HO-5 risk. This supports consistent application of the carrier’s territorial rating structure. Retain the rating support showing the territory used.

**3.C** Refer any risk with an unclear or conflicting territory assignment before completing the rating. Accurate territory selection is necessary for proper base-rate application. Document the referral and resolution in the underwriting file.

Where a row refers to the personal property limit percentage, apply the factor to the premium developed before that adjustment.

**4.B** Verify the insured location before applying the territory shown in the table. Accurate territory assignment supports consistent rating of comparable exposures. Document the location information used to confirm the territory.

**4.C** Apply approved rating adjustments after selecting the applicable base rate. The table does not replace required eligibility review or underwriting judgment. Document any adjustment applied to the base rate.

## Table 5 — Base Rates by Territory: DP-3

**5.A** Use this table to identify the base rate applicable to the rated territory for the dwelling form. The table organizes base rates by territory to support consistent rating. Document the territory selected in the rating record.

**5.B** Apply the base rate shown for the territory supported by the risk address. Accurate territory selection aligns the rating record with carrier territory definitions. Document the source used to verify the risk address and territory.

**5.C** Refer any risk with an unclear or conflicting territory assignment before applying a base rate. Unresolved territory information may produce an incorrect premium. Document the referral and the final territory determination.

Where a row refers to the unoccupancy limit, apply the factor to the premium developed before that adjustment.

## Part 6 — Deductible Factors

**6.A** Apply an AOP deductible no lower than $500.

This floor preserves the carrier's intended retained loss position.

Document the selected AOP deductible in the rating record.

**6.B** Reject any AOP deductible entered below the approved floor.

An entry below the floor produces an unauthorized premium result.

Document the correction and the source of the revised entry.

**6.C** Refer any risk when the proposed AOP deductible cannot be validated.

Unverified deductibles may not support the calculated rate.

Document the reason validation could not be completed.

**6.D** Use the deductible applicable to the coverage package being rated.

Deductible selection must align with the coverage grant used in the rate.

Document the coverage package and applied deductible.

**6.E** Apply only deductible options available in the rating system.

Unavailable options may not have approved factor support.

Document any system limitation affecting the selection.

**6.F** Do not create a custom deductible without underwriting authority.

Custom entries can alter the carrier's intended loss participation.

Document the authority supporting any approved exception.

**6.G** Confirm that the selected deductible applies to the risk location being rated.

Location-specific eligibility can affect deductible availability.

Document the location review in the rating record.

**6.H** Apply the deductible in effect for the transaction being processed.

A deductible must be rated under the applicable carrier rules.

Document the transaction basis used for the selection.

**6.I** Preserve the existing deductible when processing a change that does not alter deductible eligibility.

Unrelated changes should not create an unintended deductible revision.

Document that deductible eligibility was reviewed.

**6.J** Recalculate the premium when a deductible change is accepted.

Deductible changes affect the applicable rating factor.

Document the prior and revised deductible selections.

**6.K** Obtain underwriting review before reducing a deductible on a loss-affected risk.

Loss history can change the acceptability of a lower retained loss amount.

Document the loss review and underwriting disposition.

**6.L** Refer any request to waive a deductible.

Deductible waivers require controlled underwriting treatment.

Document the request and final disposition.

**6.M** Do not apply a deductible factor until the deductible has been confirmed.

An unconfirmed selection can create a rating error.

Document the confirmation source in the underwriting file.

**6.N** Apply the same AOP deductible basis across coverages rated on a shared deductible basis.

Inconsistent treatment can distort the intended rate.

Document any coverage that uses a separate deductible basis.

**6.O** Verify that the deductible description matches the system selection.

Narrative descriptions can conflict with coded rating entries.

Document any discrepancy and its resolution.

**6.P** Refer any risk with conflicting deductible instructions.

Conflicting instructions prevent reliable rating.

Document the conflicting information and referral outcome.

**6.Q** Apply the deductible factor generated by the rating system.

Manual substitution can bypass approved rating logic.

Document any approved override in the rating record.

**6.R** Do not retain a deductible factor after changing the deductible selection.

The factor must correspond to the final deductible.

Document that the rate was recalculated.

**6.S** Confirm deductible eligibility before binding coverage.

A deductible that is not eligible cannot support a binding decision.

Document the eligibility confirmation.

**6.T** Apply any peril-specific deductible before applying the AOP deductible to that peril.

Peril-specific treatment controls when it applies.

Document the peril classification used in rating.

**6.U** Do not use an AOP deductible for a loss subject to a separate peril deductible.

Separate peril treatment must remain distinct from AOP treatment.

Document the applicable deductible type.

**6.V** Refer any risk where the cause-of-loss coding does not identify the applicable deductible.

Unclear coding can result in incorrect deductible application.

Document the coding issue and underwriting response.

**6.W** Confirm the named storm deductible minimum before accepting a named storm deductible.

Named storm treatment requires an eligible retained loss amount.

Document the named storm deductible selected.

**6.X** Do not approve a named storm deductible that does not meet underwriting requirements.

Ineligible named storm treatment can produce an unauthorized result.

Document the underwriting decision.

**6.Y** Apply the wind deductible ceiling when reviewing wind deductible availability.

Wind deductible selection must remain within approved parameters.

Document the wind deductible review.

**6.Z** Refer any wind deductible request that exceeds the wind deductible ceiling.

Requests outside the approved range require underwriting direction.

Document the referral and disposition.

**6.AA** Confirm whether wind coverage is subject to a separate deductible.

Wind treatment may differ from the AOP deductible basis.

Document the wind deductible applicability.

**6.AB** Apply the wind deductible indicated by the approved system entry.

System selection controls the factor used for wind coverage.

Document the selected wind deductible.

**6.AC** Do not infer wind deductible eligibility from property characteristics alone.

Eligibility depends on the carrier's approved rating criteria.

Document the criteria reviewed.

**6.AD** Refer any risk with a requested wind deductible not supported by the rating system.

Unsupported selections require underwriting review.

Document the requested selection and system result.

**6.AE** Confirm whether a named storm deductible applies before rating coastal exposure.

Coastal exposure may require separate peril treatment.

Document the exposure review and deductible basis.

**6.AF** Do not apply a named storm deductible to a risk without applicable named storm coverage.

A separate deductible cannot apply without the related coverage.

Document the coverage applicability review.

**6.AG** Use the deductible associated with the final coverage territory determination.

Territory changes can affect available deductible treatment.

Document the territory used in rating.

**6.AH** Revalidate deductible eligibility when the insured location changes.

A location change can alter deductible availability.

Document the location change and revalidation.

**6.AI** Revalidate deductible eligibility when the occupancy changes.

Occupancy can affect the carrier's retained loss requirements.

Document the occupancy review.

**6.AJ** Revalidate deductible eligibility when the construction information changes.

Construction characteristics can affect available rating treatment.

Document the construction information reviewed.

**6.AK** Revalidate deductible eligibility when protection information changes.

Protection characteristics can affect deductible availability.

Document the protection information reviewed.

**6.AL** Refer any risk when system results differ from the underwriting instruction.

A difference between sources requires controlled resolution.

Document both results and the final instruction.

**6.AM** Use the most restrictive applicable deductible requirement unless underwriting directs otherwise.

Restrictive treatment avoids accepting an unauthorized retained loss position.

Document the requirement applied.

**6.AN** Do not replace a mandatory deductible with an optional deductible.

Mandatory treatment controls where the carrier requires it.

Document the mandatory deductible basis.

**6.AO** Confirm that an optional deductible was affirmatively selected.

Optional treatment must not result from an assumption.

Document the selection source.

**6.AP** Treat an absent deductible selection as incomplete rating information.

A missing selection prevents confirmation of the applicable factor.

Document the follow-up needed to complete rating.

**6.AQ** Refer any risk with a duplicate deductible entry.

Duplicate entries can cause conflicting factor application.

Document the duplicate entry and correction.

**6.AR** Remove obsolete deductible entries before finalizing the rating.

Obsolete entries can remain attached to prior coverage conditions.

Document the final deductible entries retained.

**6.AS** Confirm that the deductible is displayed consistently throughout the rating record.

Consistent display supports accurate processing and audit review.

Document any corrected display issue.

**6.AT** Do not use free-text notes as the sole support for a deductible selection.

The approved rating entry must support the applied factor.

Document the approved entry and relevant note.

**6.AU** Refer any request to apply different deductibles to similarly rated coverages.

Different treatment may require underwriting approval.

Document the requested treatment and decision.

**6.AV** Apply separate deductibles only when the rating system supports separate treatment.

Separate treatment requires approved factor logic.

Document the system support for the selection.

**6.AW** Confirm that the deductible applies to the peril shown in the loss scenario.

Deductible determination depends on the covered cause of loss.

Document the peril analysis.

**6.AX** Do not select a deductible based solely on a prior policy record.

Prior selections may not remain eligible for the current transaction.

Document the current eligibility review.

**6.AY** Review deductible selection when replacing coverage.

Replacement processing can require a new deductible determination.

Document the replacement review.

**6.AZ** Review deductible selection when rewriting coverage.

Rewrite processing can change the available rating treatment.

Document the rewrite review.

**6.BA** Preserve approved deductible treatment when renewing without a change in eligibility.

Unnecessary changes can alter the intended renewal rate.

Document the eligibility confirmation.

**6.BB** Refer any renewal where the existing deductible is no longer available.

Unavailable treatment requires underwriting direction before renewal processing.

Document the unavailable selection and disposition.

**6.BC** Confirm that the renewal deductible reflects current system availability.

System availability controls the deductible options that may be used.

Document the system review.

**6.BD** Do not apply a deductible factor from a prior transaction without recalculation.

Prior factors may not match the current selection.

Document the recalculated factor result.

**6.BE** Apply deductible changes prospectively through the approved processing method.

Controlled processing preserves accurate effective treatment.

Document the processing method used.

**6.BF** Refer any request to backdate a deductible change.

Backdated changes require underwriting review and controlled handling.

Document the request and disposition.

**6.BG** Confirm that a deductible change is supported by the insured's request or underwriting instruction.

Deductible changes require a valid processing basis.

Document the supporting source.

**6.BH** Do not alter a deductible to offset another rating characteristic.

Deductible selection must reflect approved deductible eligibility.

Document the reason for any deductible change.

**6.BI** Apply deductible factors independently from discretionary premium adjustments.

Deductible factors and discretionary adjustments serve different rating purposes.

Document each action separately.

**6.BJ** Refer any request to use a deductible as a condition of exception approval.

Exception conditions require underwriting direction.

Document the requested condition and disposition.

**6.BK** Confirm that deductible selection does not conflict with a binding restriction.

Binding restrictions can limit available deductible treatment.

Document the restriction review.

**6.BL** Do not bind a risk when the deductible remains unresolved.

An unresolved deductible prevents final rating confirmation.

Document the outstanding issue.

**6.BM** Escalate any system error that prevents deductible selection.

System errors can prevent application of approved rating logic.

Document the error and escalation result.

**6.BN** Do not estimate a deductible when the rating system is unavailable.

Estimated treatment can create an unsupported premium.

Document the delay or approved contingency direction.

**6.BO** Confirm the deductible after correcting a system error.

Correction activity can change the selected rating treatment.

Document the final system result.

**6.BP** Review the deductible whenever coverage limits are changed.

Limit changes can affect deductible eligibility or display.

Document the limit change review.

**6.BQ** Review the deductible whenever an endorsement changes covered perils.

Peril changes can affect which deductible applies.

Document the endorsement effect on deductible treatment.

**6.BR** Confirm that exclusions do not remove the peril associated with a selected separate deductible.

A separate deductible should not remain when its peril treatment is absent.

Document the exclusion review.

**6.BS** Do not apply a separate deductible to excluded coverage.

Excluded coverage does not support deductible rating treatment.

Document the coverage status.

**6.BT** Refer any risk where coverage language and system deductible treatment appear inconsistent.

Inconsistent treatment requires underwriting interpretation.

Document the inconsistency and resolution.

**6.BU** Apply deductible treatment consistently to all locations within the rated risk when required by the system.

Consistent location treatment supports the approved rating result.

Document any location-specific variation.

**6.BV** Confirm location-specific deductible treatment when locations have different exposure characteristics.

Different exposures may require different eligible selections.

Document the exposure basis for each treatment.

**6.BW** Do not aggregate deductible selections across locations unless the system directs aggregation.

Aggregation can change the intended loss participation.

Document the system direction used.

**6.BX** Complete the deductible review before releasing the final rate.

Final rates must reflect confirmed deductible treatment.

Document completion of the deductible review.

## Part 7 — Protective Device Credits

**7.A** Apply the central station discount only after validating qualifying protective-device evidence. The central station discount is 15%.

**7.B** Confirm that the protective device serves the insured location before allowing a credit. Do not apply a credit for equipment serving another premises.

Document the protected location and the evidence reviewed in the rating file.

**7.C** Require evidence that the device is installed and operational. Do not rely solely on an applicant statement where verification is available.

Operational evidence supports accurate rating and reduces unsupported credits. Document the evidence source and the device status in the rating file.

**7.D** Match the device evidence to the named insured or the insured location. Refer mismatched evidence for review before applying a credit.

Name and location matching prevents credits from being assigned to unrelated risks. Document the match or the reason for referral in the rating file.

**7.E** Verify that the protective device remains active when the risk is occupied or vacant. Do not apply a credit where the device is intentionally disabled.

An inactive device does not provide the expected loss prevention benefit. Document the reported operating condition and supporting evidence.

**7.F** Require sufficient detail to identify the protective device type. Refer vague descriptions that do not establish the protection provided.

Device identification supports consistent credit selection and audit review. Document the device description used for rating.

**7.G** Review installation evidence for signs that the device is incomplete or improperly connected. Refer evidence showing an unfinished installation.

Incomplete protection may not respond as represented at the insured location. Document the installation status and any referral action.

**7.H** Confirm that the device protects the dwelling or covered structure being rated. Do not extend a dwelling credit based on protection limited to detached property.

Protection must correspond to the exposure receiving the credit. Document the protected structure in the rating file.

**7.I** Apply protective-device credits only to eligible rating records. Refer any risk with conflicting system information.

Conflicting information may indicate inaccurate rating or unavailable protection. Document the conflict and the resolution.

**7.J** Require a clear description of the monitoring arrangement when monitoring is required for the credit. Do not infer monitoring from the presence of alarm equipment.

Alarm equipment may operate without an outside response arrangement. Document the monitoring arrangement and evidence reviewed.

**7.K** Confirm that the monitoring provider is identified in the submitted evidence. Refer evidence that does not identify the provider or service arrangement.

Provider identification supports verification of the claimed protective service. Document the provider information in the rating file.

**7.L** Verify that the protected address appears on the alarm certificate or comparable evidence. Refer any certificate showing a different address.

Address verification prevents the use of protection evidence from another location. Document the address comparison and outcome.

**7.M** Require confirmation that alarm communication is functioning where remote monitoring is claimed. Do not apply a monitored-device credit based only on installed equipment.

Remote monitoring depends on an active communication path. Document the communication confirmation in the rating file.

**7.N** Refer evidence showing that alarm signals are not transmitted to the monitoring provider. Do not allow a monitored protection credit while signal transmission is unavailable.

Unavailable transmission prevents the expected response to an alarm condition. Document the reported transmission condition and referral.

**7.O** Confirm that the device has not been removed, bypassed, or disconnected. Recalculate the rating when evidence shows protection is no longer available.

Removed or bypassed equipment does not support a protective-device credit. Document the change in device status and rating action.

**7.P** Require the insured or producer to report material changes affecting credited protection. Refer any reported change that cannot be rated from available evidence.

Changes in protection may alter eligibility for the credit. Document the reported change and the final rating treatment.

**7.Q** Review underwriting information for indications that the system is used only intermittently. Do not apply a credit where protection is not maintained as represented.

Intermittent use may not provide reliable loss prevention. Document the use condition and supporting information.

**7.R** Confirm that protective-device evidence is legible and complete. Request replacement evidence when material information cannot be read.

Unreadable evidence cannot support a reliable rating decision. Document the request and the evidence ultimately accepted.

**7.S** Accept electronic evidence when it reliably identifies the device and protected location. Refer altered or questionable electronic evidence for review.

Electronic records may support rating when authenticity and relevance are established. Document the electronic evidence source and review result.

**7.T** Do not apply a credit based on promotional material or generic product descriptions. Require evidence specific to the insured risk.

Generic material does not establish that qualifying protection exists at the location. Document the risk-specific evidence obtained.

**7.U** Verify that the device is intended to detect the hazard for which credit is sought. Do not treat unrelated equipment as qualifying protection.

Hazard-specific protection supports accurate application of rating credits. Document the hazard addressed by the device.

**7.V** Separate burglary protection from fire protection when evaluating device eligibility. Do not assume that evidence of one protection establishes the other.

Different protective functions may require different device capabilities. Document the protective function verified.

**7.W** Confirm that fire detection equipment is connected and maintained as represented. Refer any evidence of disconnected detectors or disabled notification features.

Fire protection depends on functioning detection and notification components. Document the condition of the fire protection equipment.

**7.X** Verify that burglary detection equipment protects accessible entry points as represented. Refer evidence showing limited or incomplete burglary protection.

Limited protection may not provide the loss prevention benefit expected for the credit. Document the burglary protection scope.

**7.Y** Confirm that water detection equipment is located where it can detect the represented exposure. Do not apply a water protection credit for stored or uninstalled equipment.

Placement affects whether water detection can provide meaningful loss prevention. Document the device location and exposure protected.

**7.Z** Require evidence that an automatic water shutoff device is connected to the plumbing system. Refer devices that are present but not connected or activated.

An unconnected shutoff device cannot mitigate a water loss. Document the connection status and evidence reviewed.

**7.AA** Confirm that a water shutoff device is capable of operating as represented. Refer manual-only equipment when automatic operation is required for eligibility.

Automatic response may be necessary to provide the contemplated loss mitigation. Document the operating capability used for rating.

**7.AB** Verify that smoke detection equipment is installed in the dwelling. Do not apply a credit when evidence shows detectors are absent or inoperable.

Installed, operable detectors support the intended fire loss prevention benefit. Document the detector condition and evidence source.

**7.AC** Confirm that carbon monoxide detection is not used to establish a fire protection credit. Rate each protective function only for the hazard it addresses.

Different detection functions do not provide interchangeable protection. Document the function assigned to the device.

**7.AD** Review evidence for tamper indicators, supervisory signals, or other signs of device impairment. Refer any risk where impairment is reported or apparent.

Impaired protection may fail to operate during a loss event. Document the impairment information and disposition.

**7.AE** Require clarification when the evidence identifies only a brand name or product line. Do not assume device capabilities from a brand name alone.

Product names may cover equipment with different protective features. Document the clarified device capability.

**7.AF** Verify that the system is serving the rated dwelling rather than a shared facility. Refer shared-system arrangements that do not clearly protect the insured premises.

Shared equipment may not provide the same protection to every location. Document the system arrangement and review outcome.

**7.AG** Confirm that common-area monitoring is not treated as individual dwelling protection without supporting evidence. Refer risks relying on building-wide alarm descriptions.

Building-wide services may not extend to the insured dwelling. Document the protected area shown by the evidence.

**7.AH** Require clarification where the protective-device evidence conflicts with inspection information. Do not apply the credit until the conflict is resolved.

Conflicting records may indicate that protection is not available as rated. Document the records compared and the resolution.

**7.AI** Review loss information for evidence that a credited device failed to operate as represented. Refer the risk when a failure may affect eligibility.

Reported failure may require reassessment of the credited protection. Document the failure information and referral outcome.

**7.AJ** Confirm that the device is not subject to a known service interruption. Suspend or remove the credit when protection is unavailable.

Service interruptions may eliminate the loss prevention benefit supporting the credit. Document the interruption and rating action.

**7.AK** Require updated evidence when prior evidence no longer establishes present device status. Do not continue a credit based on stale or incomplete information.

Protective-device eligibility depends on reliable current information. Document the updated evidence or the reason the credit was removed.

**7.AL** Apply a credit only after all eligibility conditions are supported in the rating file. Refer any file with missing material verification.

Complete support promotes consistent rating and defensible audit results. Document the evidence supporting each eligibility condition.

**7.AM** Do not stack protective-device credits when the same device feature supports more than one credit unless the rating logic permits it. Refer uncertain combinations for review.

Overlapping credits may overstate the protective benefit. Document the device feature and credit treatment.

**7.AN** Confirm that separate devices provide separate protective functions before recognizing more than one protective feature. Do not assume that a combined panel provides every available protection.

Combined systems may require additional components for particular functions. Document the components verified and the functions assigned.

**7.AO** Refer risks where the protective device is leased, borrowed, or otherwise not under the insured's control. Confirm that the protection will remain available to the insured location.

Control of the equipment may affect continued eligibility. Document the ownership or service arrangement.

**7.AP** Confirm that the insured location has access to the protective-device controls when operation requires insured action. Refer arrangements controlled solely by another party.

Lack of access may prevent the device from being used as represented. Document the control arrangement and final disposition.

**7.AQ** Require evidence of restoration when prior information showed the device was impaired. Do not reinstate a credit based only on a statement that repairs were made.

Restoration must be supported before protective value can be recognized. Document the repair evidence and reinstatement decision.

**7.AR** Verify that equipment shown in photographs is installed at the insured location. Refer photographs that do not establish location or operating status.

Photographs alone may not establish that protection is available to the risk. Document the image review and any corroborating evidence.

**7.AS** Review device certificates for limitations or exclusions affecting the claimed protection. Refer certificates that describe a narrower protection than the rating request.

Certificate limitations may affect whether the device qualifies for credit. Document the limitation and rating treatment.

**7.AT** Require clarification when a certificate identifies an alarm account without describing the protected service. Do not infer protective features from account status alone.

Account status does not establish the type of protection provided. Document the service description used for rating.

**7.AU** Confirm that the monitoring arrangement includes the represented alarm response. Refer evidence that shows notification without monitored response.

Notification arrangements may differ from monitored protective services. Document the response arrangement verified.

**7.AV** Do not rely on an alarm yard sign, decal, or similar visual indicator as sole evidence of protection. Require documentation of the actual device and service.

Visual indicators may remain after equipment or service is discontinued. Document the additional evidence obtained.

**7.AW** Verify that the credited device is not limited to a portion of the dwelling that excludes the principal exposure. Refer partial-protection arrangements for review.

Partial protection may not meet the intended eligibility standard. Document the protected area and disposition.

**7.AX** Confirm that protective equipment is compatible with the dwelling's occupancy and construction. Refer arrangements that may not function reliably in the reported environment.

Environmental conditions may affect device performance and availability. Document the compatibility review.

**7.AY** Require explanation of any protective-device information supplied after a loss. Refer the risk when post-loss information conflicts with prior records.

Post-loss information may require additional scrutiny before affecting rating. Document the information received and review outcome.

**7.AZ** Confirm that a credited system is not in testing, installation, or repair status. Do not apply a credit while normal protective operation is unavailable.

Temporary operating conditions may prevent the system from providing expected protection. Document the reported status and rating action.

**7.BA** Review the risk file for duplicate protective-device records. Use the record that most clearly supports the current rating decision.

Duplicate records may contain inconsistent or outdated information. Document the record selected and any discrepancy resolved.

**7.BB** Require a clear link between the device evidence and the policy location. Refer evidence that identifies only a mailing address or general account address.

A mailing address may not establish the location receiving protection. Document the location linkage used for rating.

**7.BC** Confirm that monitored protection is not credited when the monitoring account is closed. Remove the credit when closure is verified.

A closed account does not provide the represented monitoring service. Document the account status and rating adjustment.

**7.BD** Refer any risk where the insured reports repeated false alarms or unreliable device operation. Evaluate whether the reported condition affects credit eligibility.

Unreliable operation may reduce the expected loss prevention value. Document the report and review determination.

**7.BE** Verify that the device evidence supports the specific coverage location being rated. Do not extend a credit across locations without separate support.

Protection at one location does not establish protection at another location. Document the location-specific evidence.

**7.BF** Confirm that protective-device information is consistent with occupancy information. Refer a risk when occupancy conditions may affect device use or monitoring.

Occupancy conditions may affect whether protective equipment remains active. Document the occupancy review and disposition.

**7.BG** Require clarification when the insured describes self-monitoring instead of professional monitoring. Do not treat self-monitoring as a central station arrangement.

Self-monitoring may not provide the same alarm handling or response process. Document the monitoring type confirmed.

**7.BH** Verify that the central station service applies to the insured location. Refer service evidence that does not identify the protected premises.

Central station service must be tied to the rated risk. Document the service-location match.

**7.BI** Confirm that the device is capable of sending an alarm signal when the claimed credit requires signal transmission. Refer equipment limited to local audible warning.

Local warning equipment may not provide the represented remote protection. Document the signal capability verified.

**7.BJ** Require explanation when device evidence identifies a discontinued service provider. Do not assume service continues after a provider change.

Provider changes may affect monitoring continuity and device operation. Document the provider status and evidence reviewed.

**7.BK** Refer any protective-device submission that appears altered, incomplete, or internally inconsistent. Do not cure material inconsistencies through assumption.

Questionable evidence cannot support an unverified credit. Document the concern and referral outcome.

**7.BL** Confirm that the protective device is used for loss prevention rather than convenience only. Do not apply a credit to equipment lacking a qualifying protective function.

Convenience equipment may not reduce the insured hazard. Document the qualifying function established.

**7.BM** Verify that device maintenance information does not show unresolved defects. Refer risks with reported defects that could impair protection.

Unresolved defects may prevent the device from responding to a loss event. Document the maintenance information and disposition.

**7.BN** Require a documented basis for every protective-device credit entered in the rating system. Do not leave a credit unsupported by file evidence.

Supported entries promote accurate processing and audit review. Document the evidence reference in the rating file.

**7.BO** Confirm that the credit selected matches the verified protective feature. Refer any rating entry that cannot be tied to the evidence.

An incorrect credit selection may misstate the premium. Document the verified feature and rating entry.

**7.BP** Review rating changes caused by device additions, removals, or impairments. Apply the change when the updated information is verified.

Device status changes may affect eligibility for protective-device credits. Document the change and resulting rating action.

**7.BQ** Require clarification when a device is described as smart-home equipment without a stated protective function. Do not assign a credit based on smart-home capability alone.

Smart-home equipment may perform functions unrelated to loss prevention. Document the protective function confirmed.

**7.BR** Confirm that a monitored fire alarm is not credited when only emergency dispatch equipment is shown. Require evidence of qualifying fire detection and monitoring.

Dispatch equipment alone may not detect a fire condition. Document the detection and monitoring evidence.

**7.BS** Verify that a burglary system includes active detection components when burglary protection is claimed. Refer systems limited to cameras, lighting, or access convenience features.

Related security equipment may not provide qualifying alarm protection. Document the detection components verified.

**7.BT** Confirm that water protection devices are not credited when they only report consumption or usage data. Require evidence of leak detection or shutoff capability.

Usage monitoring may not prevent or mitigate water damage. Document the water protection function established.

**7.BU** Refer risks where the protective device depends on utilities that are unavailable at the location. Confirm that the device can operate as represented.

Unavailable utilities may prevent protective equipment from functioning. Document the utility condition and review result.

**7.BV** Verify that backup capability is present when the evidence claims uninterrupted protective operation. Do not infer backup capability from standard equipment descriptions.

Backup arrangements may affect whether protection remains available during an interruption. Document the capability supported by evidence.

**7.BW** Confirm that notification contacts or response arrangements are active when required by the credited service. Refer evidence showing inactive or missing response contacts.

Response arrangements may be necessary for monitored protection to provide value. Document the response information reviewed.

**7.BX** Require clarification when the device evidence is issued for a commercial account but the risk is residential. Refer any mismatch in account purpose or protected premises.

Account classification may indicate that the evidence does not apply to the rated dwelling. Document the account review and disposition.

**7.BY** Do not apply a protective-device credit solely because a device was required by a lender, landlord, or local authority. Verify that the device independently meets credit eligibility.

Required installation does not establish qualifying protective performance. Document the evidence of qualifying operation.

**7.BZ** Confirm that an inspection finding is reconciled before retaining a conflicting protective-device credit. Refer unresolved inspection discrepancies.

Inspection findings may provide direct evidence of device condition. Document the inspection finding and resolution.

**7.CA** Require the rating file to show who reviewed protective-device evidence. Record the reviewer action when a credit is applied, changed, or declined.

Reviewer accountability supports consistent underwriting administration. Document the reviewer identification and action taken.

**7.CB** Use internal carrier terminology when recording protective-device decisions. Do not quote this manual direction to an insured.

Internal language preserves the procedural purpose of this manual. Document the rating rationale in carrier records.

**7.CC** Refer any risk requiring an exception from standard protective-device eligibility. Do not apply an exception without documented approval.

Exceptions require controlled review to maintain consistent rating practice. Document the approval and supporting rationale.

**7.CD** Confirm that the rating system entry agrees with the completed protective-device review. Correct any mismatch before finalizing the transaction.

System accuracy is necessary for proper premium calculation and audit support. Document the correction or confirmation in the rating file.

**7.CE** Retain the evidence supporting the protective-device decision in the carrier record. Ensure that the file can explain the credit without relying on memory.

A complete record supports later review and consistent servicing. Document the evidence retained and its source.

**7.CF** Reassess protective-device eligibility when reliable information indicates a material change. Do not continue a credit after verified loss of qualifying protection.

Verified changes may eliminate the basis for the credit. Document the reassessment and rating result.

**7.CG** Refer uncertainty about device eligibility instead of making assumptions. Apply a credit only when the available information supports the decision.

Assumptions can create unsupported premium adjustments. Document the uncertainty and referral disposition.

**7.CH** Confirm that all protective-device decisions are consistent with the evidence in the file. Correct unsupported entries promptly when identified.

Consistent file support promotes reliable rating administration. Document the review performed and corrective action.

**7.CI** Complete the protective-device review before closing the rating transaction. Do not leave eligibility questions unresolved in the active file.

Unresolved questions may result in an inaccurate credit. Document the final eligibility determination and supporting evidence.

## Part 8 — Roof Credits and Surcharges

**8.1** Apply a new roof premium credit of 20% only when the submitted evidence supports completed roof replacement. Refer incomplete or conflicting evidence for underwriting review.

Completed replacement supports the intended risk classification. Partial work may not improve the condition of the entire roof system.

Document the evidence reviewed in the rating file. Identify the roof areas confirmed as replaced.

**8.2** Verify the roof covering type before applying any roof credit or surcharge. Rate the risk using the covering type supported by the file.

Roof performance depends on the installed covering and its condition. Unsupported material descriptions can produce inaccurate rating.

Document the verified covering type in the rating record. Retain the supporting inspection material or applicant evidence.

**8.3** Refer any risk with more than one roof covering type when the file does not identify the location of each covering. Do not assume that the most favorable covering applies to the entire risk.

Mixed roof systems may present different susceptibility to damage. Clear location details are necessary for consistent rating.

Document the location and condition of each identified covering. Record the basis for the applied rating treatment.

**8.4** Confirm that replacement work applies to the insured dwelling before recognizing a roof credit. Do not apply a dwelling credit based on work performed on an outbuilding.

Roof improvements must relate to the rated structure. Improvements to another structure do not change the dwelling exposure.

Document the structure to which the replacement work applies. Retain evidence linking the work to the insured dwelling.

**8.5** Refer any risk when the roof evidence identifies work that is not clearly replacement, repair, or maintenance. Do not classify ambiguous work as a replacement.

Repairs and maintenance may improve localized conditions without changing the overall roof exposure. Accurate work classification supports sound rating.

Document the description of work shown by the available evidence. State why the file was referred.

**8.6** Apply a surcharge when the roof condition indicates increased susceptibility to loss. Use only the surcharge treatment supported by the confirmed condition.

Visible deterioration can increase the likelihood or severity of water, wind, and related damage. The rating record must reflect the actual observed exposure.

Document the condition observations supporting the surcharge. Retain photographs when available.

**8.7** Refer any risk with active leakage, visible interior staining, or reported water intrusion. Do not remove a surcharge solely because the exterior covering appears serviceable.

Interior evidence may indicate a roof-system failure not apparent from exterior observations. Underwriting review is required when loss potential remains unclear.

Document the reported or observed water condition. Identify the source of the information.

**8.8** Verify that roof replacement evidence identifies the insured location. Do not accept evidence that cannot be reasonably associated with the risk.

Documents from another property do not establish roof condition at the insured location. Location verification protects rating integrity.

Document how the evidence was matched to the risk. Retain the identifying property details in the file.

**8.9** Require sufficient evidence to establish that claimed roof work has been completed. Do not apply a credit based only on a proposal, estimate, or contract for future work.

Planned work does not alter the present exposure. Rating must reflect the condition in effect for the applicable transaction.

Document the completion evidence reviewed. Record any limitations in the available evidence.

**8.10** Refer any risk when replacement evidence conflicts with inspection observations. Do not disregard a material discrepancy without an underwriting determination.

Conflicting evidence may indicate incomplete work, incorrect records, or a condition requiring further review. Resolution is necessary before final rating.

Document each conflicting item in the file. Record the underwriting disposition.

**8.11** Evaluate roof condition as a system and not solely by the appearance of the field covering. Consider visible condition at edges, penetrations, valleys, and drainage areas.

Roof failures may arise from components other than the primary covering. System-level review improves consistency in credit and surcharge decisions.

Document the observed roof-system conditions. Note any area that could not be observed.

**8.12** Refer any risk with missing, lifted, curled, cracked, or displaced roof covering. Apply no favorable treatment until the condition is resolved or underwritten.

These conditions may indicate reduced resistance to weather-related damage. They can also signal broader deterioration beneath the covering.

Document the specific condition and its apparent extent. Retain available photographs or inspection notes.

**8.13** Apply no roof credit when the evidence supports only localized patching. Refer the risk if the patching suggests unresolved roof deterioration.

Localized repairs do not necessarily restore the condition of the overall roof. Repeated repairs may indicate an elevated loss exposure.

Document the observed or reported patching. State the basis for the rating action.

**8.14** Refer any risk with visible moss, algae, vegetation, or debris that may impair roof drainage or condition. Do not treat surface growth as cosmetic when it obscures the roof condition.

Surface accumulation can retain moisture and conceal deterioration. It may also prevent reliable evaluation of the roof system.

Document the affected areas and any visibility limitations. Record whether additional evidence was requested.

**8.15** Confirm whether a roof inspection was limited by height, access, weather, obstruction, or safety conditions. Refer the risk when the limitation prevents reliable condition assessment.

An incomplete inspection may not reveal material roof defects. Rating assumptions must not replace verified information.

Document the inspection limitation. Identify the information used to complete the rating decision.

**8.16** Use underwriting review when satellite imagery, inspection findings, and applicant statements do not agree. Do not select the most favorable source without support.

Different sources may reflect different observation conditions or property dates. Review is needed to establish the reliable description of risk.

Document each source considered. Record the reason for the selected rating basis.

**8.17** Refer any risk with evidence of roof damage attributed to a prior event that has not been repaired. Apply the rating treatment supported by the current condition.

Unrepaired damage may increase vulnerability to subsequent loss. Prior-event information is relevant only when it describes the present exposure.

Document the observed damage and reported repair status. Retain available supporting information.

**8.18** Do not apply a roof credit where the roof covering is installed over material that prevents reliable confirmation of condition. Refer the risk when the layering affects underwriting acceptability.

Multiple covering layers can conceal deterioration and complicate repair assessment. The condition of underlying components may remain unknown.

Document the observed layering or reported installation method. State the underwriting action taken.

**8.19** Refer any risk when the roof deck appears sagged, uneven, or structurally compromised. Do not apply favorable roof treatment pending review.

Deck irregularities may indicate moisture damage, structural concerns, or installation deficiencies. These conditions can materially affect loss potential.

Document the location and appearance of the irregularity. Retain photographs or inspection comments when available.

**8.20** Verify that the roof covering extends to all material dwelling areas before applying a whole-dwelling credit. Refer the risk when an addition or enclosed area has a different roof condition.

Different roof areas may present different exposures within the same dwelling. Whole-dwelling treatment requires support for the entire rated structure.

Document each distinct roof area and its condition. Record the basis for the applied credit or surcharge.

**8.21** Refer any risk with an unfinished roof replacement project. Do not apply a completed-roof credit while temporary protection or exposed components remain.

Unfinished work can create elevated water and wind exposure. The completed condition cannot be established until the work is finalized.

Document the visible stage of work. Record the underwriting referral and any requested evidence.

**8.22** Do not apply a credit based solely on the presence of a contractor name, manufacturer name, or material label. Verify that the evidence supports actual installation at the insured location.

Product identification does not establish completed work or roof condition. Rating requires a clear connection between the product and the risk.

Document the evidence establishing installation. Retain material labels only as supplemental support.

**8.23** Refer any risk when roof replacement evidence is altered, incomplete, or internally inconsistent. Do not rely on questionable evidence without underwriting approval.

Unreliable documentation can lead to unsupported credits or missed surcharges. File integrity is necessary for accurate rating.

Document the deficiency identified. Record the source of any replacement evidence accepted.

**8.24** Apply a surcharge only when the condition supporting it is material to roof exposure. Do not apply a surcharge for an unsupported or purely speculative concern.

Surcharges must reflect a confirmed increase in expected loss potential. Consistent application supports fair and defensible rating.

Document the material condition supporting the surcharge. State the evidence used to confirm it.

**8.25** Remove a roof surcharge only after evidence supports correction of the condition that caused it. Refer the risk when the evidence does not establish that the condition has been resolved.

A prior adverse condition may remain present after limited or unrelated work. Removal requires support for the current roof condition.

Document the corrective work or inspection finding. Record the reason for removing the surcharge.

**8.26** Refer any risk when the roof has visible temporary coverings, tarps, unsecured sheeting, or emergency repairs. Do not apply a roof credit while temporary measures remain in place.

Temporary protection may indicate unresolved damage or incomplete repairs. It can also obscure the underlying roof condition.

Document the temporary measure and affected area. Retain available photographs and inspection notes.

**8.27** Confirm that reported roof replacement includes the primary weather barrier. Do not apply a replacement credit for cosmetic work that does not affect roof performance.

Cosmetic improvements may not reduce the roof exposure. The credit is intended for work that supports the relevant risk characteristic.

Document the scope of completed work. Record the evidence showing the weather barrier was addressed.

**8.28** Refer any risk with deteriorated flashing, exposed fasteners, damaged penetrations, or failed sealant. Evaluate the roof system for related water-entry exposure.

These components are common sources of roof-related water damage. Their condition can affect the performance of an otherwise sound covering.

Document the affected component and location. State whether the condition supports referral or surcharge treatment.

**8.29** Do not infer roof condition from the condition of neighboring properties. Rate the insured dwelling based on evidence specific to the insured risk.

Nearby homes may have different maintenance histories, construction details, or storm damage. Property-specific evidence is required for defensible rating.

Document the evidence obtained for the insured dwelling. Do not retain unsupported neighborhood assumptions as a rating basis.

**8.30** Refer any risk with evidence that the roof covering is nearing the roof age from which the actual cash value roof schedule applies. Do not represent schedule treatment as a roof credit or surcharge decision.

Roof valuation treatment and roof condition rating address different underwriting considerations. Clear handling avoids inconsistent file conclusions.

Document the evidence relevant to the roof age from which the actual cash value roof schedule applies. Record the underwriting review outcome.

**8.31** Refer any risk when the reported roof condition approaches the maximum insurable roof age. Do not bind favorable roof treatment without the required underwriting determination.

Roof age may affect eligibility and loss exposure even when visible deterioration is limited. Review ensures that the risk remains within carrier appetite.

Document the evidence relevant to the maximum insurable roof age. Record the referral disposition in the rating file.

**8.32** Confirm that the applicant's description of roof work is supported by independent evidence when available. Refer the risk when the description is the only evidence and material rating treatment is requested.

Applicant statements can be useful but may not describe the scope or completion of work accurately. Corroboration improves rating reliability.

Document the applicant statement and all supporting evidence. State whether the evidence was independently verified.

**8.33** Refer any risk with a roof covering that appears incompatible with the observed roof slope or design. Do not assume proper installation from appearance alone.

Installation characteristics can affect drainage, wind resistance, and water shedding. A potential mismatch requires underwriting evaluation.

Document the observed slope or design concern. Retain supporting inspection material when available.

**8.34** Apply roof treatment only after resolving whether a detached structure is included in the dwelling rating basis. Do not extend dwelling roof treatment to separate structures without support.

Detached structures may have separate exposures and rating treatment. Clear classification prevents unintended credit or surcharge application.

Document the structure classification used. Record the roof condition for each relevant structure.

**8.35** Refer any risk with visible hail impact, wind damage, or storm-created openings. Do not treat weather-related damage as ordinary wear without supporting evidence.

Recent or unrepaired storm damage can materially increase the likelihood of water intrusion. The source of damage may affect underwriting disposition.

Document the observed damage pattern and reported cause. Retain photographs when available.

**8.36** Require clear roof photographs when photographs are used to support a credit or surcharge. Refer the risk when image quality prevents reliable assessment.

Blurred, distant, or obstructed images may not show relevant roof conditions. Reliable imagery supports consistent file decisions.

Document the source and quality of the photographs. Identify any condition that could not be confirmed.

**8.37** Do not apply favorable roof treatment when substantial roof areas are obscured by trees, snow, debris, or equipment. Obtain additional evidence or refer the risk.

Obstructed areas may conceal damage, deterioration, or incomplete work. A partial view may not represent the entire roof condition.

Document the source of the obstruction. Record the action taken to resolve the visibility limitation.

**8.38** Refer any risk with roof runoff directed toward walls, foundations, openings, or vulnerable building components. Consider whether the drainage condition affects roof-related loss exposure.

Improper drainage can contribute to water damage even when the roof covering is serviceable. The roof system includes its ability to shed water away from the dwelling.

Document the observed drainage pattern. State the basis for the underwriting referral or rating action.

**8.39** Verify that gutter, downspout, and drainage observations are not used as substitutes for roof condition evidence. Use them only as supporting information.

Drainage components may indicate maintenance quality but do not independently establish roof integrity. The rating basis must remain tied to confirmed roof conditions.

Document the roof condition evidence separately from drainage observations. Retain both when relevant to the decision.

**8.40** Refer any risk when roof replacement appears to have been performed without consistent workmanship. Do not apply a credit when installation quality cannot be reasonably supported.

Poor workmanship can negate the expected benefit of new materials. Visible installation concerns may indicate increased future loss potential.

Document the workmanship concern and its location. Record the evidence reviewed by underwriting.

**8.41** Do not apply a credit for replacement of only an accessory roof area. Refer the risk if the accessory area condition suggests related issues with the main roof.

Accessory areas may be exposed to different construction or maintenance conditions. Their replacement does not establish the condition of the primary dwelling roof.

Document the accessory area involved. State whether the main roof condition was separately verified.

**8.42** Refer any risk when the roof covering has visible granular loss, surface erosion, or exposed substrate. Do not offset these conditions with unrelated maintenance evidence.

Material loss from the covering can reduce weather resistance. Unrelated improvements do not resolve the observed roof exposure.

Document the affected roof areas and apparent severity. Retain available inspection evidence.

**8.43** Confirm that roof evidence is current enough to describe the risk being rated. Refer the risk when material changes may have occurred after the evidence was created.

Roof condition can change because of weather, repairs, or deterioration. Stale evidence may not support the present rating decision.

Document why the evidence was accepted as representative. Record any indication of later roof changes.

**8.44** Refer any risk when a prior inspection notes roof concerns that are absent from current evidence without explanation. Do not presume that an undocumented condition has been corrected.

Conditions may remain despite changed descriptions or incomplete records. Resolution protects against unsupported removal of adverse treatment.

Document the prior concern and the current evidence reviewed. Record the explanation accepted or the referral made.

**8.45** Apply no roof credit when the file identifies material roof concerns but lacks evidence of repair or replacement. Refer the risk for underwriting direction.

Unresolved concerns may continue to affect the probability of loss. Favorable rating treatment requires support for the current condition.

Document the unresolved concern. State the evidence missing from the file.

**8.46** Refer any risk with a roof configuration that creates concealed valleys, dead zones, or difficult-to-drain areas. Obtain evidence sufficient to assess the affected condition.
