Complex configurations can retain water and accumulate debris. Limited visibility may conceal deterioration in vulnerable areas.

Document the configuration concern and affected location. Record any inspection limitations.

**8.47** Do not treat roof cleaning as roof replacement or material repair. Refer the risk when cleaning reveals damage or cannot establish the underlying condition.

Cleaning may improve appearance without improving the weather barrier. It can also expose defects previously hidden by surface accumulation.

Document the cleaning evidence and observed roof condition. State whether additional review was required.

**8.48** Refer any risk when roof-mounted equipment, solar components, antennas, or similar installations affect roof visibility or condition. Do not assume the underlying roof is sound.

Roof-mounted items can create penetrations, conceal damage, or complicate repair assessment. Underwriting review may be needed to evaluate the resulting exposure.

Document the installed item and its apparent effect on the roof. Retain available photographs or inspection notes.

**8.49** Verify that a claimed replacement roof has not been materially damaged after installation. Refer the risk when post-installation damage is reported or observed.

A replacement roof may still present elevated exposure if later damage remains unresolved. Completion of prior work does not eliminate current condition concerns.

Document the reported or observed later damage. Record the evidence supporting the final treatment.

**8.50** Apply consistent roof treatment to risks with comparable supported conditions. Refer exceptions rather than creating unsupported individual practices.

Consistent application promotes reliable pricing and underwriting discipline. Exceptions require a documented business rationale.

Document the condition comparison used. Record the underwriting approval for any exception.

**8.51** Refer any risk when roof condition evidence appears to describe only a limited elevation. Do not extend that observation to unobserved roof areas without support.

Condition can vary by exposure, drainage pattern, and maintenance history. A partial observation may not represent the whole roof.

Document the observed elevation and unobserved areas. Record the evidence used for the final rating action.

**8.52** Do not apply a surcharge solely because a roof is weathered in appearance. Confirm that the observed condition supports an increased roof exposure.

Normal weathering does not always indicate functional impairment. Surcharge application requires a material condition basis.

Document the specific functional concern supporting the surcharge. Retain the related inspection evidence.

**8.53** Refer any risk with signs of animal activity, nesting, or damage at roof edges, vents, or eaves. Evaluate whether the condition may permit water entry or further deterioration.

Animal activity can damage roof components and conceal openings. The condition may also indicate deferred maintenance.

Document the observed activity or damage. State the underwriting action taken.

**8.54** Confirm that replacement evidence distinguishes between roof covering work and work on non-roof exterior components. Do not apply roof treatment based on siding, fascia, or trim work alone.

Exterior work may improve appearance without changing roof performance. The scope of work must support the requested roof treatment.

Document the component replaced or repaired. Record how the roof scope was verified.

**8.55** Refer any risk when a roof inspection identifies unsafe access or conditions that prevent adequate review. Do not request unsafe inspection practices to obtain rating evidence.

Safety limitations can leave material roof areas unverified. Underwriting must determine whether alternative evidence is sufficient.

Document the access limitation and alternative evidence considered. Record the referral outcome.

**8.56** Apply a credit or remove a surcharge only after confirming that the applicable roof condition has been addressed across the affected area. Do not rely on repair evidence that leaves material damage unresolved.

Partial correction may not reduce the overall exposure. The rating action must align with the condition remaining after work is completed.

Document the corrected and unresolved areas. State the basis for the final treatment.

**8.57** Refer any risk with evidence of repeated roof repairs in the same area. Do not assume repeated repairs have permanently resolved the underlying cause.

Recurring repairs may indicate persistent water entry, installation failure, or structural movement. Further review is needed to assess the present exposure.

Document the repair history described in the file. Retain available evidence of the recurring condition.

**8.58** Confirm that the roof condition reported by a contractor is consistent with other file evidence. Refer the risk when the contractor statement lacks supporting detail.

Contractor statements may reflect a limited scope of observation or a repair recommendation. Independent support improves reliance on the statement.

Document the contractor statement and its stated basis. Record any corroborating evidence.

**8.59** Refer any risk with a roof covering that is visibly loose at ridges, hips, edges, or transitions. Do not apply favorable treatment before the condition is evaluated.

Loose roof components may allow wind-driven rain and progressive damage. Edge and transition conditions can be especially vulnerable.

Document the location and type of looseness observed. Retain photographs when available.

**8.60** Do not use claim history alone to determine a roof credit or surcharge. Use claim information only with evidence of the current roof condition.

Claim history may identify areas requiring review but does not establish present condition. Current evidence remains necessary for rating action.

Document the current evidence reviewed with any claim information. State the condition that supports the final treatment.

**8.61** Refer any risk where the roof replacement evidence does not identify the work scope with reasonable clarity. Do not infer full replacement from a general description.

General descriptions may include repair, maintenance, or limited replacement work. Scope clarity is necessary before recognizing a credit.

Document the wording of the submitted evidence. Record any clarification obtained.

**8.62** Apply surcharge treatment only to the extent supported by the risk condition and carrier rating rules. Refer any circumstance that does not fit established treatment.

Unsupported expansion of surcharge treatment can produce inconsistent rating. Underwriting review is required for unusual conditions.

Document the condition and the selected treatment. Record the reason for any referral.

**8.63** Refer any risk when roof replacement or repair work appears to have created exposed openings, unsealed penetrations, or incomplete transitions. Do not apply a credit until the roof system is complete.

Incomplete transitions can permit water entry despite new roof materials. The roof must perform as a connected weather barrier.

Document the incomplete component and its location. Retain available photographs or inspection notes.

**8.64** Maintain roof credit and surcharge evidence in the rating file. Ensure that the file explains both the condition observed and the action taken.

A complete file supports audit, quality review, and consistent future handling. It also allows another reviewer to understand the rating basis.

Document the final roof classification and supporting evidence. Record the reviewer responsible for the decision.

**8.65** Refer any risk not clearly addressed by the available roof evidence or established rating treatment. Do not create an unsupported roof credit or surcharge outcome.

Unusual roof conditions require underwriting judgment within carrier appetite. Referral prevents inconsistent handling of uncertain exposures.

Document the issue requiring referral. Record the underwriting instruction applied to the risk.

## Part 9 — Windstorm Mitigation Credits

**9.A** Apply an opening protection credit of 35% when verified opening protection meets carrier requirements. Do not apply the credit when evidence is incomplete.

Opening protection reduces expected wind-borne debris damage. Unsupported credit treatment distorts the rated premium.

Record the evidence source and protection description in the rating file. Retain the material relied upon for the credit.

**9.B** Verify that opening protection serves the rated structure before applying credit. Disregard protection serving detached or unrated property.

Protection must reduce damage potential at the insured location. Protection elsewhere does not support the rated exposure.

Document the protected openings and their location. Note any excluded structures or openings.

**9.C** Confirm that submitted evidence identifies the protected opening. Do not infer protection from a general statement of compliance.

Opening-specific evidence supports consistent rating treatment. General assertions may not establish the scope of protection.

Record the opening identification used for review. Retain the submitted evidence in the file.

**9.D** Require evidence that protection was in place at the time credit is applied. Do not credit planned, ordered, or incomplete work.

Only installed protection affects the current wind exposure. Future work does not support current rating treatment.

Document the observed installation status. Record any pending work that prevented credit.

**9.E** Review exterior doors for qualifying protection where the credit depends on protected openings. Treat unprotected exterior doors as material to eligibility.

Exterior doors may admit wind and debris damage. A protected window does not cure an exposed door.

Document the door protection reviewed. Describe any unprotected exterior door affecting the result.

**9.F** Review windows for qualifying protection where the credit depends on protected openings. Treat damaged or missing components as unsupported protection.

Windows are common points of wind-borne debris loss. Defective components may fail when needed.

Document the window protection condition. Retain clear evidence of the reviewed openings.

**9.G** Review glazed openings in garage doors when they affect opening protection eligibility. Do not assume garage door protection extends to glazed areas.

Glazed areas may have different resistance characteristics. Separate review avoids overstating protection.

Document the garage door construction and glazed opening treatment. Record the basis for the rating decision.

**9.H** Confirm that shutter systems are designed for the opening they protect. Do not accept loosely stored panels without supporting installation evidence.

A shutter system must perform as an installed protective measure. Unmatched components may not protect the opening.

Document the shutter type and mounting arrangement. Retain evidence showing the system matched to the opening.

**9.I** Confirm that panel systems include the required attachment components. Do not credit panels that cannot be secured to the structure.

Panels without suitable attachment may not remain in place during wind conditions. Attachment capability is part of the protective system.

Document the attachment method and visible hardware. Note missing or unsuitable components.

**9.J** Review permanent impact-resistant glazing as opening protection when supported by acceptable evidence. Do not rely on ordinary glazing descriptions.

Impact-resistant glazing may reduce breach potential from debris. Ordinary glazing does not establish equivalent protection.

Document the glazing designation and evidence source. Retain photographs or records supporting the determination.

**9.K** Treat removable protection as eligible only when the submitted evidence establishes a complete system. Do not credit isolated components.

Removable systems depend on compatible panels, hardware, and storage. Partial systems may leave openings exposed.

Document the components reviewed and their condition. Record any missing element that affected eligibility.

**9.L** Verify that protection is capable of covering the full opening. Do not credit protection that leaves material exposure at the opening perimeter.

Partial coverage may allow wind or debris intrusion. Complete opening protection supports the intended mitigation result.

Document the opening dimensions and coverage observed. Describe any uncovered area.

**9.M** Review roof openings and skylight assemblies when they are part of the submitted mitigation evidence. Do not assume roof-mounted glazing is protected by wall-opening features.

Roof openings have distinct exposure to wind-driven debris. Wall protection may not address roof vulnerabilities.

Document the roof opening type and protection reviewed. Retain evidence supporting the treatment applied.

**9.N** Confirm that evidence is attributable to the rated address. Do not accept evidence from another location without clear location verification.

Location-specific evidence prevents transfer of mitigation credit between risks. Similar structures may have different protective features.

Document the address verification method. Identify any discrepancy in the submitted material.

**9.O** Use current evidence that reasonably reflects the condition of the protection. Refer stale or unclear evidence for underwriting review.

Protection condition may change through damage, alteration, or removal. Current evidence supports reliable rating.

Document the evidence date or condition indicator. Record the reason for any referral.

**9.P** Refer evidence that conflicts with observed property characteristics. Do not resolve material inconsistencies by assumption.

Conflicting evidence may indicate an error or a changed exposure. Underwriting review preserves rating integrity.

Document the conflicting items and the referral action. Retain each item used in the review.

**9.Q** Confirm that the protection is installed on the dwelling rather than merely stored at the location. Do not treat stored materials as installed protection without supporting evidence.

Stored materials may be unavailable, incomplete, or unsuitable for use. Installed protection offers more reliable loss mitigation.

Document whether the protection is installed or stored. Describe the evidence supporting that conclusion.

**9.R** Treat protection for occupied portions of the rated structure separately from protection for excluded portions. Do not extend credit beyond the verified protected exposure.

Different portions of a structure may have different wind vulnerability. Rating must reflect the actual protected area.

Document the portions reviewed and their protection status. Note any excluded portion affecting eligibility.

**9.S** Confirm that protective devices are maintained in serviceable condition. Do not credit visibly damaged, warped, corroded, or inoperable devices.

Condition affects whether a device can perform during a wind event. Defects may defeat otherwise qualifying construction.

Document observed condition concerns. Retain photographs when available.

**9.T** Refer any risk with altered openings when the submitted evidence predates the alteration. Do not apply credit until protection is verified for the altered condition.

Altered openings may not match prior protective features. Prior evidence may no longer describe the exposure.

Document the alteration and the evidence gap. Record the referral outcome.

**9.U** Review combination protection systems as a complete assembly. Do not credit a combination system when its components are incompatible.

A system performs only when its components work together. Incompatible features may leave the opening unprotected.

Document the components and their compatibility. Record the basis for accepting or declining credit.

**9.V** Treat decorative shutters as nonqualifying unless evidence establishes protective performance. Do not infer mitigation value from appearance.

Decorative shutters may not resist wind-borne debris. Appearance alone does not establish protective capability.

Document the shutter construction and supporting evidence. Record why the feature was accepted or declined.

**9.W** Evaluate the wind percentage deductible minimum separately from mitigation eligibility. Do not change deductible treatment because a mitigation credit applies.

Deductible requirements address retained loss exposure. Mitigation credits address verified protective characteristics.

Document the deductible treatment used for the risk. Record the mitigation review independently.

**9.X** Treat the hours after a named storm designation ends that the named storm period lasts as a claims-period concept, not a mitigation rating input. Do not alter credit treatment based on storm-period status.

Storm handling concepts do not establish protective construction. Rating treatment depends on verified mitigation features.

Document mitigation evidence without relying on storm-period status. Record any question requiring claims or underwriting review.

**9.Y** Verify that submitted photographs are clear enough to identify the protection. Do not credit features that cannot be reasonably evaluated from the image.

Unclear images may conceal material defects or incomplete installation. Reliable visual evidence supports consistent treatment.

Document the photographs reviewed and the features visible. Note any image limitation affecting eligibility.

**9.Z** Confirm that photographs depict the rated structure. Do not rely on generic, stock, or unidentified images.

Images must support the actual insured exposure. Unidentified images do not establish property-specific protection.

Document the basis for matching images to the structure. Retain identifying image details in the file.

**9.AA** Review inspection reports for consistency with the submitted mitigation evidence. Refer material differences for underwriting review.

Inspection findings may reveal conditions not shown elsewhere. Consistent evidence supports accurate credit treatment.

Document the report reviewed and any differences found. Record the referral disposition.

**9.AB** Accept contractor statements only when they clearly describe the installed protection. Do not rely on vague statements of wind resistance.

Contractor statements vary in detail and reliability. Clear descriptions permit meaningful verification.

Document the contractor statement and described feature. Record any missing information.

**9.AC** Confirm that the evidence identifies the relevant construction feature rather than a general renovation. Do not credit renovation work without mitigation support.

Renovation may not include qualifying wind protection. General improvement descriptions do not establish eligibility.

Document the construction feature relied upon. Retain supporting records for the feature.

**9.AD** Refer protection evidence with altered, obscured, or incomplete content. Do not use evidence that cannot be reasonably authenticated.

Evidence integrity is necessary for rating reliability. Altered content may misstate the protected condition.

Document the integrity concern and referral action. Retain the questioned material.

**9.AE** Verify that the protection applies to accessible exterior openings. Do not treat interior barriers as opening protection.

Exterior protection addresses the initial breach path. Interior barriers may not prevent wind-driven entry.

Document the location of the protective feature. Record why it was treated as exterior or interior.

**9.AF** Confirm that protection remains available for the opening after ordinary operation of the structure. Do not credit devices that are permanently blocked or inaccessible.

Protection must be usable when wind conditions arise. Inaccessible devices may not provide practical mitigation.

Document the access condition observed. Describe any obstruction affecting eligibility.

**9.AG** Review evidence of repairs when repaired protection supports credit. Do not assume repaired components meet carrier requirements without verification.

Repairs may restore, alter, or reduce protective performance. Verification prevents unsupported credit continuation.

Document the repair evidence and resulting condition. Record any limitation identified during review.

**9.AH** Treat replacement openings as unverified until supporting evidence confirms their protection. Do not carry prior credit to replacement openings automatically.

Replacement work may change the protective characteristics of the opening. Prior construction may not match the replacement.

Document the replacement opening and evidence reviewed. Record whether credit continued or was removed.

**9.AI** Confirm that the rated construction type matches the mitigation evidence. Refer discrepancies involving opening configuration or material.

Construction differences may affect how protection performs. Matching evidence supports appropriate credit treatment.

Document the construction information reviewed. Record each discrepancy requiring referral.

**9.AJ** Review attached structures only when they are included in the rated exposure. Do not extend dwelling credit to separate property without rating support.

Attached and separate structures may have different exposure treatment. Credit must follow the rated interest.

Document the structure relationship and rating treatment. Note any structure excluded from credit consideration.

**9.AK** Verify that protective panels are identifiable to the opening system when identification is available. Do not assume interchangeable panels are suitable.

Panel fit may determine whether protection remains secure. Unidentified panels may not provide reliable coverage.

Document the panel identification reviewed. Record any fit concern affecting eligibility.

**9.AL** Confirm that locking or fastening mechanisms are functional when they support the protection system. Do not credit nonfunctioning mechanisms.

Fastening mechanisms help protective devices remain in place. Failure may expose the opening during wind conditions.

Document the mechanism condition observed. Retain evidence of any defect.

**9.AM** Review sliding and hinged openings for protection appropriate to their operation. Do not assume fixed-opening treatment applies to moving openings.

Moving openings may require distinct securing features. Operating components can affect protective performance.

Document the opening operation and protection method. Record the basis for the eligibility decision.

**9.AN** Treat screened enclosures as separate from the primary dwelling opening protection review. Do not credit screening as impact protection without supporting evidence.

Screening may not resist wind-borne debris. Primary dwelling openings require their own verification.

Document the screened area and its relationship to the dwelling. Record any protection treated separately.

**9.AO** Verify that protection is not dependent on missing or unavailable components. Do not credit systems awaiting replacement hardware or panels.

Unavailable components may prevent deployment during a wind event. Complete systems support dependable mitigation.

Document the unavailable component and resulting treatment. Record any underwriting referral.

**9.AP** Review evidence from remote inspection sources with appropriate caution. Refer material questions that cannot be resolved from the available material.

Remote evidence may omit condition details or viewing angles. Additional review may be needed for reliable rating.

Document the remote source and limitations observed. Record the resolution or referral.

**9.AQ** Confirm that protective features were not removed after evidence was created. Do not continue credit when removal is known or reported.

Removed protection no longer reduces current loss potential. Rating must reflect the present condition.

Document the removal information and effective rating action. Retain related correspondence or evidence.

**9.AR** Treat temporary coverings as nonqualifying unless carrier requirements specifically support their use. Do not equate emergency coverings with permanent mitigation.

Temporary coverings may not withstand expected wind conditions. Permanent mitigation requires reliable performance.

Document the covering type and condition. Record why credit was declined or referred.

**9.AS** Verify that protection applies across the dwelling elevation where required by the submitted basis. Do not rely on evidence from a limited view without support for remaining openings.

A limited view may not show material unprotected openings. Broader verification supports accurate exposure assessment.

Document the elevations reviewed and any limitations. Record the evidence supporting complete treatment.

**9.AT** Confirm that opening protection evidence is internally consistent. Refer mismatched descriptions, images, or inspection findings.

Consistent records strengthen confidence in the mitigation determination. Mismatches may indicate incomplete or incorrect information.

Document the inconsistency and referral details. Retain the conflicting evidence.

**9.AU** Review loss information for indications that protection was damaged or compromised. Do not assume credit remains supported after a relevant loss.

Loss events may affect protective features. Post-loss condition may differ from prior evidence.

Document the loss information considered and resulting action. Record any request for updated evidence.

**9.AV** Treat ongoing construction as an unverified condition until protection is complete and supported. Do not apply credit during unfinished installation.

Construction activity may leave openings exposed or systems incomplete. Finished condition must be verified before rating.

Document the construction status and available evidence. Record the reason credit was deferred.

**9.AW** Confirm that mitigation evidence identifies the property owner, applicant, or authorized representative when required for validation. Refer evidence with unresolved ownership concerns.

Authorization concerns may affect the reliability of submitted information. Valid evidence supports sound underwriting treatment.

Document the source and authority of the submission. Record any validation concern.

**9.AX** Review application statements against available evidence before applying credit. Do not rely on statements contradicted by inspection or image material.

Application information may be incomplete or outdated. Evidence-based review prevents incorrect credit.

Document the application statement and corroborating material. Record any discrepancy.

**9.AY** Confirm that the protective feature is intended for wind mitigation rather than another hazard. Do not credit security devices without wind-performance support.

Security features may not resist wind-borne debris. Hazard-specific performance is necessary for mitigation credit.

Document the feature purpose and supporting evidence. Record the rationale for the treatment applied.

**9.AZ** Refer any risk where the evidence does not permit a clear eligibility decision. Do not create a credit by estimating protective performance.

Uncertain conditions require underwriting judgment. Estimation may produce inconsistent rating results.

Document the unanswered question and referral action. Retain the evidence available at the time of review.

**9.BA** Review changes in occupancy or use when they affect access to protective devices. Refer conditions that may impair deployment or maintenance.

Use of the property can affect practical availability of mitigation features. Impaired access may reduce expected protection.

Document the occupancy or use concern. Record the referral disposition.

**9.BB** Confirm that protective devices have not been modified without supporting evidence. Do not assume altered devices retain their prior performance.

Modification may affect fit, attachment, or resistance. Prior approval may not apply to the altered condition.

Document the modification observed or reported. Record the evidence used to resolve eligibility.

**9.BC** Treat mixed protection types according to the verified protection at each opening. Do not assume the strongest feature applies throughout the structure.

Mixed systems may leave some openings more vulnerable than others. Opening-level review supports accurate credit treatment.

Document the protection type by opening group. Record any unprotected or unsupported condition.

**9.BD** Verify that evidence shows the protection in a usable configuration when configuration affects performance. Do not rely solely on closed, stored, or partial views.

Configuration may determine whether a device provides effective coverage. Limited views may not establish functionality.

Document the configuration reviewed and evidence limitations. Record the basis for acceptance or referral.

**9.BE** Review maintenance records when they are submitted to support continued credit. Do not treat maintenance records as proof of protection type without corroboration.

Maintenance records may show care but not construction characteristics. Corroborating evidence supports proper classification.

Document the maintenance information reviewed. Identify the evidence confirming the protective feature.

**9.BF** Confirm that feature labels or markings correspond to the installed opening. Do not rely on unattached labels or packaging.

Labels may be separated from the relevant product. Installed-condition evidence is more reliable.

Document the label or marking and its location. Record how it was matched to the opening.

**9.BG** Refer properties with unusual opening shapes or configurations when standard evidence is insufficient. Do not assume standard protection fits a nonstandard opening.

Unusual configurations may affect coverage and attachment. Specialized review may be necessary.

Document the unusual configuration and evidence provided. Record the referral outcome.

**9.BH** Review protection for openings below covered porches or similar features separately. Do not assume overhead coverage provides debris protection.

Overhead features may not shield an opening from wind-driven debris. Opening protection remains subject to verification.

Document the overhead feature and opening protection. Record the treatment applied.

**9.BI** Confirm that exterior opening protection remains attached to the insured structure. Do not credit freestanding or loosely positioned barriers.

Attached protection is more likely to remain effective during wind conditions. Loose barriers may fail or become debris.

Document the attachment location and method. Retain evidence supporting the determination.

**9.BJ** Treat evidence of manufacturer performance as support only when it corresponds to the installed product. Do not apply generic product information to an unidentified installation.

Product performance depends on the actual installed component. Generic materials may not describe the risk.

Document the product information and installation match. Record any uncertainty requiring referral.

**9.BK** Verify that protection does not depend on an unconfirmed manual action when evidence shows no practical means of deployment. Do not credit unusable systems.

A system must be capable of use during a wind event. Impractical deployment may defeat expected mitigation.

Document the deployment method and access condition. Record the basis for the decision.

**9.BL** Review exterior wall openings created by additions or conversions. Do not rely on evidence from the original structure for added openings.

Additions and conversions may introduce unprotected exposure. Original evidence may not cover changed construction.

Document the added or converted area. Record the protection evidence for its openings.

**9.BM** Confirm that protective features are not limited to a display area or model unit. Do not extend model evidence to the rated dwelling.

Model features may differ from installed features. The rating file must support the actual risk.

Document the connection between evidence and the rated dwelling. Record any limitation found.

**9.BN** Refer evidence that suggests differing protection levels among similar openings without explanation. Do not assume consistent treatment across those openings.

Differences may reflect incomplete installation or varying construction. Review avoids an unsupported broad credit.

Document the differing conditions and referral details. Retain the related evidence.

**9.BO** Treat statements from property representatives as supplemental evidence when independent verification is available. Do not rely on unsupported verbal descriptions.

Representative statements may be incomplete or based on assumption. Independent evidence supports consistent rating.

Document the statement and corroborating material. Record any unresolved issue.

**9.BP** Verify that the protective feature has not been rendered ineffective by later exterior work. Do not continue credit when later work obstructs or alters the system.

Exterior work may affect fit, access, or attachment. Current conditions control mitigation eligibility.

Document the later work and observed effect. Record the resulting rating treatment.

**9.BQ** Review seasonal storage practices when removable protection is relied upon. Refer conditions showing improper storage or missing components.

Storage conditions may affect availability and integrity. Damaged or missing components may impair performance.

Document the storage condition and component availability. Record any referral action.

**9.BR** Confirm that opening protection is evaluated independently from roof condition. Do not grant opening credit based on roof mitigation features.

Roof and opening features address different loss pathways. Each feature requires its own evidence.

Document the opening evidence separately from roof information. Record the applicable rating treatment.

**9.BS** Confirm that opening protection is evaluated independently from anchorage features. Do not grant opening credit based on structural anchorage evidence.

Anchorage addresses structural movement rather than opening breach. Separate evaluation preserves proper classification.

Document the opening protection evidence reviewed. Record any unrelated mitigation feature separately.

**9.BT** Treat missing evidence as an absence of support for credit. Do not use prior assumptions when the rating file lacks verification.

The carrier must be able to support the mitigation determination. Missing evidence prevents reliable confirmation.

Document the missing item and resulting action. Record any request for additional information.

**9.BU** Review evidence submitted after a rating change for completeness. Do not revise credit treatment until the new evidence is validated.

Later submissions may correct or complicate prior information. Validation prevents improper retroactive treatment.

Document the received evidence and review result. Record the rating action taken.

**9.BV** Refer suspected misrepresentation involving mitigation features. Do not discuss suspected fraud conclusions in routine rating notes.

Suspected misrepresentation requires controlled handling. Routine notes should preserve factual underwriting observations.

Document the factual inconsistency and referral action. Avoid unsupported characterizations.

**9.BW** Confirm that mitigation evidence is legible and complete before relying on it. Do not reconstruct missing details from assumption.

Legible records permit consistent review and audit. Incomplete records may omit material conditions.

Document the material reviewed and any legibility issue. Record the action taken to resolve it.

**9.BX** Apply mitigation credit only through approved rating handling. Do not override the rating result to accommodate premium expectations.

Credit must reflect verified risk characteristics. Premium considerations do not replace evidence-based eligibility.

Document the approved rating treatment. Retain the evidence supporting the credit decision.

**9.BY** Review cancellation or nonrenewal information for indications that mitigation evidence may be outdated. Refer material concerns before continuing credit.

Changes in risk status may correspond with changed property conditions. Updated review protects rating accuracy.

Document the information considered and resulting action. Record any request for current evidence.

**9.BZ** Confirm that underwriting exceptions are clearly authorized before applying nonstandard credit treatment. Do not create an exception through informal communication.

Exception handling requires controlled authority. Informal direction may not support the rating file.

Document the authorization and exception rationale. Retain the approval evidence.

**9.CA** Maintain factual, neutral notes when documenting mitigation review. Do not include opinions that are unsupported by the evidence.

Neutral notes preserve a clear underwriting record. Unsupported opinions may impair later review.

Document observed facts, evidence sources, and actions taken. Record referrals without speculation.

**9.CB** Retain sufficient evidence to reproduce the mitigation decision. Do not remove supporting material while the credit remains in effect.

Reproducible decisions support audit, service, and claim review. Retained evidence demonstrates compliance with carrier practice.

Document the final eligibility determination and evidence location. Confirm that the rating file supports the applied treatment.

## Table 10 — Endorsement Premiums

**10.A** Use the endorsement premium table to identify the applicable premium for each eligible endorsement. The table supports consistent rating and prevents unsupported premium selection. Document the endorsement applied and the premium selected in the rating record.

**10.B** Verify that the endorsement description, risk characteristics, and rating basis match the table entry before applying premium. Accurate matching ensures the endorsement charge reflects the exposure accepted. Document any underwriting review or exception in the file.

Where a row refers to the sewer backup limit, apply the factor to the premium developed before that adjustment.

Where a row refers to the mold coverage limit, apply the factor to the premium developed before that adjustment.

Where a row refers to the code upgrade percentage, apply the factor to the premium developed before that adjustment.

Where a row refers to the extended replacement cost percentage, apply the factor to the premium developed before that adjustment.

Where a row refers to the seismic deductible, apply the factor to the premium developed before that adjustment.

Where a row refers to the identity theft expense limit, apply the factor to the premium developed before that adjustment.

## Table 11 — Increased Limits Factors

**11.A** Use the table to identify the increased limits factor applicable to the selected coverage limit. Apply the factor only to the rating basis shown in the table.

The table maintains consistent rating treatment when increased limits are selected. Do not interpolate, combine, or otherwise modify a listed factor.

Document the selected coverage limit, applicable factor, and resulting rating treatment in the underwriting file.

**11.B** Refer any submission for which no listed increased limits factor applies. Do not bind using an assumed or estimated factor.

The table is limited to the increased limits combinations approved for manual rating. Exceptions require underwriting review before issuance or renewal.

Document the referral reason and underwriting disposition in the underwriting file.

Where a row refers to the jewelry theft sublimit, apply the factor to the premium developed before that adjustment.

Where a row refers to the association assessment limit, apply the factor to the premium developed before that adjustment.

Where a row refers to the card and forgery limit, apply the factor to the premium developed before that adjustment.

## Part 12 — State Exception Pages

**12.A** Verify the named insured and risk location before rating.  
This preserves account integrity.  
Document the verified data in the file.  

**12.B** Confirm occupancy before assigning a homeowners rating basis.  
This supports the correct eligibility review.  
Document the occupancy evidence in the file.  

**12.C** Confirm construction class before applying territory factors.  
This supports consistent rating treatment.  
Document the construction basis in the file.  

**12.D** Verify the dwelling use before accepting the risk.  
This identifies risks requiring referral.  
Document the dwelling use in the file.  

**12.E** Refer any risk with unclear ownership.  
This prevents unsupported eligibility decisions.  
Document the referral outcome in the file.  

**12.F** Verify the mailing address when it differs from the risk location.  
This supports accurate policy administration.  
Document the verified address information in the file.  

**12.G** Confirm that the risk location is within an eligible territory.  
This preserves territory rating integrity.

**12.H** Refer any risk that exceeds the coastal territory wind maximum.  
This requires underwriting review of wind exposure.  
Document the referral decision in the file.

This supports accurate mitigation treatment.  
Document the exposure review in the file.

Document the supporting evidence in the file.  

**12.K** Do not apply an opening protection credit without acceptable verification.  
This prevents unsupported premium adjustment.  
Document the verification status in the file.

This prevents credit application to unrelated features.

Document the conflicting information in the file.

**12.GF** Refer unsatisfied underwriting conditions.  
This requires further review.  
Document the outstanding conditions in the file.  

**12.GG** Confirm that adverse information has been evaluated.  
This supports sound underwriting judgment.  
Document the evaluation in the file.  

**12.GH** Refer material adverse information not previously reviewed.  
This requires underwriting review.  
Document the information in the file.  

**12.GI** Verify that the risk remains eligible at issuance.  
This supports final quality control.  
Document the eligibility review in the file.  

**12.GJ** Refer any change that affects eligibility.  
This requires underwriting review before issuance.  
Document the change in the file.  

**12.GK** Confirm that the issued policy reflects approved rating.  
This supports premium accuracy.  
Document the final review in the file.  

**12.GL** Correct discrepancies before release.  
This prevents issuance error.  
Document the correction in the file.  

**12.GM** Verify that the policy file supports all underwriting decisions.  
This supports audit readiness.  
Document the file review in the file.  

**12.GN** Refer unsupported underwriting decisions.  
This requires corrective review.  
Document the referral decision in the file.  

**12.GO** Confirm that state exception treatment is complete before issuance.  
This supports consistent administration.  
Document the completion review in the file.  

**12.GP** Refer unresolved state exception issues.  
This requires underwriting direction.  
Document the issue in the file.  

**12.GQ** Verify that all required evidence is legible.  
This supports reliable file review.  
Document the evidence review in the file.  

**12.GR** Obtain legible replacement evidence when needed.  
This preserves file integrity.  
Document the replacement evidence in the file.  

**12.GS** Confirm that file notes identify material decisions.  
This supports auditability.  
Document the decision basis in the file.  

**12.GT** Record the reason for each referral outcome.  
This supports consistent underwriting administration.  
Document the outcome in the file.  

**12.GU** Verify that approved exceptions are clearly identified.  
This supports controlled exception handling.  
Document the exception approval in the file.  

**12.GV** Do not apply an exception without recorded approval.  
This preserves underwriting authority.  
Document the approval status in the file.  

**12.GW** Confirm that the final file is complete.  
This supports accurate policy issuance.  
Document the completion review in the file.  

**12.GX** Refer incomplete final files.  
This prevents unsupported issuance.  
Document the missing items in the file.  

**12.GY** Verify that all material risk changes are reflected in rating.  
This supports premium accuracy.  
Document the rating review in the file.  

**12.GZ** Refer material changes not reflected in rating.  
This requires corrective underwriting review.  
Document the change in the file.  

**12.HA** Confirm that the risk description remains accurate at issuance.  
This supports policy accuracy.  
Document the final risk description in the file.  

**12.HB** Correct inaccurate risk descriptions before release.  
This prevents policy administration error.  
Document the correction in the file.  

**12.HC** Verify that all referral records are retained.  
This supports audit readiness.  
Document the record review in the file.  

**12.HD** Retain the completed underwriting file with the policy record.  
This supports consistent carrier administration.  
Document completion of the retention action in the file.
